### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following are true about the energy of the given d-orbitals of a tetrahedral complex?
A. $d_{xy} = d_{xz} > d_{x^2-y^2}$
B. $d_{xy} = d_{yz} > d_{z^2}$
C. $d_{x^2-y^2} > d_{z^2} > d_{xz}$
D. $d_{x^2-y^2} = d_{z^2} < d_{xz}$

Choose the correct answer from the given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, B and D only
- **(B)** A and B only
- **(C)** B and D only
- **(D)** B, C and D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) A, B and D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a tetrahedral complex, the five d-orbitals split into two sets due to the crystal field created by the ligands.

The ligands approach the central metal ion between the axes. The $d_{xy}$, $d_{yz}$, and $d_{xz}$ orbitals (forming the $t_2$ set) point between the axes, experiencing greater repulsion from the ligands, and thus have higher energy.

The $d_{x^2-y^2}$ and $d_{z^2}$ orbitals (forming the $e$ set) point along the axes, experiencing lesser repulsion, and thus have lower energy.

Therefore, the energy order is $d_{xy} = d_{yz} = d_{xz} > d_{x^2-y^2} = d_{z^2}$.

Evaluating the given statements:
A. $d_{xy} = d_{xz} > d_{x^2-y^2}$ is true.
B. $d_{xy} = d_{yz} > d_{z^2}$ is true.
C. $d_{x^2-y^2} > d_{z^2} > d_{xz}$ is false.
D. $d_{x^2-y^2} = d_{z^2} < d_{xz}$ is true.

Hence, statements A, B, and D are true.

Answer: A, B and D only

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Match the LIST-I with LIST-II
<table style="border: 1px solid black; border-collapse: collapse; width: auto;"><tr><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List-I
Electronic configuration of tetrahedral metal ion</th><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List-II
Crystal Field Stabilization Energy ($\Delta_t$)</th></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">A. $d^2$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">I. $-0.6$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">B. $d^4$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">II. $-0.8$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">C. $d^6$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">III. $-1.2$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">D. $d^8$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">IV. $-0.4$</td></tr></table>
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-III, B-IV, C-II, D-I
- **(B)** A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II
- **(C)** A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II
- **(D)** A-II, B-I, C-IV, D-III

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a tetrahedral crystal field, the d-orbitals split into a lower energy $e$ set and a higher energy $t_2$ set. The energy of an electron in the $e$ orbital is $-0.6 \Delta_t$ and in the $t_2$ orbital is $+0.4 \Delta_t$. Since $\Delta_t$ is generally small, tetrahedral complexes form high-spin configurations.

The Crystal Field Stabilization Energy (CFSE) is given by:
$\text{CFSE} = (-0.6 n_e + 0.4 n_{t_2}) \Delta_t$

For $d^2$: The configuration is $e^2 t_2^0$.
$\text{CFSE} = 2 \times (-0.6) + 0 = -1.2 \Delta_t$ (A $\rightarrow$ III)

For $d^4$: The configuration is $e^2 t_2^2$.
$\text{CFSE} = 2 \times (-0.6) + 2 \times (0.4) = -1.2 + 0.8 = -0.4 \Delta_t$ (B $\rightarrow$ IV)

For $d^6$: The configuration is $e^3 t_2^3$.
$\text{CFSE} = 3 \times (-0.6) + 3 \times (0.4) = -1.8 + 1.2 = -0.6 \Delta_t$ (C $\rightarrow$ I)

For $d^8$: The configuration is $e^4 t_2^4$.
$\text{CFSE} = 4 \times (-0.6) + 4 \times (0.4) = -2.4 + 1.6 = -0.8 \Delta_t$ (D $\rightarrow$ II)

Therefore, the correct match is A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II.

Answer: A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The total number of unpaired electrons present in the $d^3$, $d^4$ (low spin), $d^5$ (high spin), $d^6$ (high spin) and $d^7$ (low spin) octahedral complex systems is _______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **15**

**Detailed Solution:**

In an octahedral complex, the d-orbitals split into $t_{2g}$ and $e_g$ levels.

For $d^3$: Configuration is $t_{2g}^3 e_g^0$. Number of unpaired electrons = $3$.

For $d^4$ (low spin): Configuration is $t_{2g}^4 e_g^0$. Number of unpaired electrons = $2$.

For $d^5$ (high spin): Configuration is $t_{2g}^3 e_g^2$. Number of unpaired electrons = $5$.

For $d^6$ (high spin): Configuration is $t_{2g}^4 e_g^2$. Number of unpaired electrons = $4$.

For $d^7$ (low spin): Configuration is $t_{2g}^6 e_g^1$. Number of unpaired electrons = $1$.

Total number of unpaired electrons = $3 + 2 + 5 + 4 + 1 = 15$.

Answer: $15$

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements:
Statement I: Each electron in $e_g$ orbitals destabilizes the orbitals by $+0.6\Delta_o$ and each electron in the $t_{2g}$ orbitals stabilizes the orbitals by $-0.4\Delta_o$ in an octahedral field on the basis of crystal field theory.
Statement II: All the d-orbitals of the transition metals have the same energy in their free atomic state but when a complex is formed the ligands destroy the degeneracy of these orbitals on the basis of crystal field theory.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect
- **(C)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- **(D)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct**

**Detailed Solution:**

In an octahedral crystal field, the five degenerate d-orbitals split into two sets: a lower energy $t_{2g}$ set and a higher energy $e_g$ set.

The energy of the $t_{2g}$ orbitals is lowered by $0.4\Delta_o$ (stabilization of $-0.4\Delta_o$) and the energy of the $e_g$ orbitals is raised by $0.6\Delta_o$ (destabilization of $+0.6\Delta_o$) relative to the barycenter. Hence, Statement I is correct.

In the free atomic or ionic state, all five d-orbitals of a transition metal are degenerate (have the same energy). When ligands approach the central metal ion to form a complex, the spherically symmetrical field is disrupted, which destroys the degeneracy of the d-orbitals and causes them to split. Hence, Statement II is correct.

Both Statement I and Statement II are correct.

Answer: Both Statement I and Statement II are correct

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct statements about metal carbonyls are
A. The metal-carbon bonds in metal carbonyls possess both $\sigma$ and $\pi$-character.
B. Due to synergic bonding interactions between metal and CO ligand, the metal-carbon bond becomes weak.
C. The metal-carbon $\sigma$ bond is formed by the donation of lone pair of electrons on the carbonyl carbon into a vacant orbital of metal.
D. The metal-carbon $\pi$ bond is formed by the donation of electrons from filled d-orbital of metal into vacant $\pi^*$ orbital of CO.
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and B Only
- **(B)** A, C and D Only
- **(C)** B and C Only
- **(D)** A and D Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A, C and D Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement A is correct. The metal-carbon bond in metal carbonyls possesses both $\sigma$ and $\pi$ character due to synergic bonding.

Statement B is incorrect. The synergic bonding interaction between the metal and the CO ligand strengthens the metal-carbon bond.

Statement C is correct. The metal-carbon $\sigma$ bond is formed by the donation of a lone pair of electrons from the carbonyl carbon into a vacant orbital of the metal.

Statement D is correct. The metal-carbon $\pi$ bond is formed by the back-donation of electrons from a filled d-orbital of the metal into the vacant antibonding $\pi^{*}$ orbital of the CO ligand.

Therefore, statements A, C, and D are correct.

Answer: A, C and D Only

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Match List - I with List - II.
<table style="border: 1px solid black; border-collapse: collapse; width: auto;"><tr><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List - I
Chromium (III) Complexes
(en = ethylene diamine)</th><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List - II
$\Delta_0$ (cm$^{-1}$)</th></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">A. $[Cr(CN)_6]^{3-}$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">I. $15,060$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">B. $[CrF_6]^{3-}$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">II. $17,400$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">C. $[Cr(H_2O)_6]^{3+}$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">III. $22,300$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">D. $[Cr(en)_3]^{3+}$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">IV. $26,600$</td></tr></table>
Choose the <b>correct</b> answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-I, B-II, C-III, D-IV
- **(B)** A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I
- **(C)** A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II
- **(D)** A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III**

**Detailed Solution:**

The crystal field splitting energy $\Delta_0$ depends on the strength of the ligand.

According to the spectrochemical series, the order of ligand strength is $F^- < H_2O < en < CN^-$.

Thus, the order of $\Delta_0$ for the given complexes is $[CrF_6]^{3-} < [Cr(H_2O)_6]^{3+} < [Cr(en)_3]^{3+} < [Cr(CN)_6]^{3-}$.

Matching with the given values:
$[CrF_6]^{3-}$ has the minimum $\Delta_0 = 15,060$ cm$^{-1}$ (B $\rightarrow$ I).
$[Cr(H_2O)_6]^{3+}$ has $\Delta_0 = 17,400$ cm$^{-1}$ (C $\rightarrow$ II).
$[Cr(en)_3]^{3+}$ has $\Delta_0 = 22,300$ cm$^{-1}$ (D $\rightarrow$ III).
$[Cr(CN)_6]^{3-}$ has the maximum $\Delta_0 = 26,600$ cm$^{-1}$ (A $\rightarrow$ IV).

This corresponds to A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III.

Answer: A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The number of isoelectronic species among $\mathrm{Sc}^{3+}, \mathrm{Cr}^{2+}, \mathrm{Mn}^{3+}, \mathrm{Co}^{3+}$ and $\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}$ is ' $\mathrm{n}^{\prime}$. If ' $\mathrm{n}^{\prime}$ moles of AgCl is formed during the reaction of complex with formula $\mathrm{CoCl}_{3}(\mathrm{en})_{2} \mathrm{NH}_{3}$ with excess of $\mathrm{AgNO}_{3}$ solution, then the number of electrons present in the $t_{2 g}$ orbital of the complex is $\_\_\_\_$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the complex CoCl₃(en)₂NH₃, cobalt is in +3 oxidation state with electron configuration [Ar]3d⁶.
In an octahedral field with strong field ligands (ethylenediamine, ammonia, chloride), Co³⁺ forms a low-spin complex with $t_{2g}^6 e_g^0$ configuration.
The $t_{2g}$ orbitals consist of $d_{xy}$, $d_{xz}$, and $d_{yz}$.
With all 6 electrons filling the three $t_{2g}$ orbitals, the total number of electrons in the $t_{2g}$ orbital set is 6.

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The wavelength of light absorbed for the following complexes are in the order \(\underset{\text{(I)}}{\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+}} ;\underset{\text{(II)}}{\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{3+}} ;\underset{\text{(III)}}{\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}} ;\underset{\text{(IV)}}{\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)\right]^{3+}} ;\underset{\text{(V)}}{\left[\mathrm{CoF}_6\right]^{3-}}\)

**Options:**

- **(A)** III $<$ I $<$ IV $<$ V $<$ II
- **(B)** III $<$ I $<$ IV $<$ II $<$ V
- **(C)** III $<$ I $<$ II $<$ IV $<$ V
- **(D)** III $<$ IV $<$ I $<$ II $<$ V

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) III $<$ I $<$ IV $<$ II $<$ V**

**Detailed Solution:**

Wavelength of light absorbed increases as C.F.S.E of complex decreases.
$[Co(CN)_6]^{3-}$ has maximum CFSE
$[CoF_6]^{3-}$ has least CFSE
Ligand field strength $\uparrow$ ; C.F.S.E $\uparrow$
Correct wavelength order.
$V > II > IV > I > III$

</details>

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The crystal field splitting energy of $\left[\mathrm{Co}(\text { oxalate })_{3}\right]^{3-}$ complex is ' $\mathrm{n}^{\prime}$ times that of the $\left[\mathrm{Cr}(\text { oxalate })_{3}\right]^{3-}$ complex. Here ' $\mathrm{n}^{\prime}$ is $\_\_\_\_$. (Assume $\Delta_{0} \gg \mathrm{P}$)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

The Crystal Field Stabilization Energy (CFSE) is calculated based on the electronic configuration of the central metal ion in the octahedral field.
For $[Co(ox)_3]^{3-}$:
Cobalt is in $+3$ oxidation state. $Co^{3+}$ has a $d^6$ configuration.
Given $\Delta_0 \gg P$, it is a low spin complex. The electrons fill the $t_{2g}$ level first.
Configuration: $t_{2g}^6 e_g^0$.
CFSE for $Co^{3+} = 6 \times (-0.4 \Delta_0) + 0 \times (0.6 \Delta_0) = -2.4 \Delta_0$.
For $[Cr(ox)_3]^{3-}$:
Chromium is in $+3$ oxidation state. $Cr^{3+}$ has a $d^3$ configuration.
Configuration: $t_{2g}^3 e_g^0$.
CFSE for $Cr^{3+} = 3 \times (-0.4 \Delta_0) + 0 \times (0.6 \Delta_0) = -1.2 \Delta_0$.
Comparing the magnitudes of the stabilization energies:
Magnitude of CFSE for $Co^{3+} = 2.4 \Delta_0$.
Magnitude of CFSE for $Cr^{3+} = 1.2 \Delta_0$.
The ratio $n = \frac{2.4 \Delta_0}{1.2 \Delta_0} = 2$.

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements : 
 <b> Statement I</b>: $\quad\left[\mathrm{CoBr}_{4}\right]^{2-}$ ion will absorb light of lower energy than $\left[\mathrm{CoCl}_{4}\right]^{2-}$ ion. 
 <b>Statement II</b> : In $\left[\mathrm{CoI}_{4}\right]^{2-}$ ion, the energy separation between the two set of d-orbitals is more than $\left[\mathrm{CoCl}_{4}\right]^{2-}$ ion. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(C)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement I is true but Statement II is false**

**Detailed Solution:**

Crystal field theory explains d-d transitions in complexes based on ligand field strength.
Statement I: Ligand field strength order is I⁻ < Br⁻ < Cl⁻ < F⁻. Since Br⁻ is weaker than Cl⁻, [CoBr₄]²⁻ has smaller crystal field splitting (Δ) than [CoCl₄]²⁻. The d-d absorption energy equals the splitting energy, so [CoBr₄]²⁻ absorbs lower energy light. TRUE
Statement II: I⁻ is a much weaker field ligand than Cl⁻. Therefore, [CoI₄]²⁻ has smaller crystal field splitting than [CoCl₄]²⁻, not larger. The energy separation in [CoI₄]²⁻ is less than in [CoCl₄]²⁻. FALSE
Statement I is true but Statement II is false.

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

$\left[\mathrm{Ni}\left(\mathrm{PPh}_{3}\right)_{2} \mathrm{Cl}_{2}\right]$ is a paramagnetic complex. Identify the INCORRECT statements about this complex. 
 A. The complex exhibits geometrical isomerism. 
 B. The complex is white in colour. 
 C. The calculated spin-only magnetic moment of the complex is 2.84 BM . 
 D. The calculated CFSE (Crystal Field Stabilization Energy) of Ni in this complex is $-0.8 \Delta_{\mathrm{o}}$. 
 E. The geometrical arrangement of ligands in this complex is similar to that in $\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_{4}$. 
 Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** C, D and E Only
- **(B)** C and D Only
- **(C)** A, B and D Only
- **(D)** A and B Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) A, B and D Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

In $[Ni(PPh_3)_2Cl_2]$, Nickel is in $+2$ oxidation state. $Ni^{2+}$ has a $3d^8$ electronic configuration.
The complex is given as paramagnetic. For a 4-coordinate $d^8$ system, paramagnetism implies a tetrahedral geometry ($sp^3$ hybridization) with two unpaired electrons.
Statement A: Tetrahedral complexes of the type $[MA_2B_2]$ do not exhibit geometrical isomerism because all positions are adjacent to each other. Thus, A is incorrect.
Statement B: Most $Ni(II)$ complexes are colored due to $d-d$ transitions. $[Ni(PPh_3)_2Cl_2]$ is a dark blue/green crystalline solid, not white. Thus, B is incorrect.
Statement C: With 2 unpaired electrons, the spin-only magnetic moment $\mu = \sqrt{n(n+2)} = \sqrt{2(2+2)} = \sqrt{8} \approx 2.83$ BM. Thus, C is correct.
Statement D: For a tetrahedral complex with $d^8$ configuration, the CFSE is calculated as $CFSE = [(-0.6 \times 4) + (0.4 \times 4)]\Delta_t = -0.8\Delta_t$. The statement mentions $-0.8\Delta_o$, which is incorrect as the complex is tetrahedral, not octahedral. Thus, D is incorrect.
Statement E: $Ni(CO)_4$ is a tetrahedral complex ($sp^3$ hybridization). Since $[Ni(PPh_3)_2Cl_2]$ is also tetrahedral, their geometrical arrangements are similar. Thus, E is correct.
The incorrect statements are A, B, and D.

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the transition metal ions $\mathrm{Mn}^{3+}, \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}, \mathrm{Fe}^{3+}$ and $\mathrm{Co}^{3+}$ and all form low spin octahedral complexes. The correct decreasing order of unpaired electrons in their respective d-orbitals of the complexes is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Mn}^{3+}>\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}>\mathrm{Co}^{3+}>\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}>\mathrm{Mn}^{3+}>\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}>\mathrm{Co}^{3+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}>\mathrm{Co}^{3+}>\mathrm{Mn}^{3+}>\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}>\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}>\mathrm{Co}^{3+}>\mathrm{Mn}^{3+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}>\mathrm{Mn}^{3+}>\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}>\mathrm{Co}^{3+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For low spin octahedral complexes, electrons fill the $t_{2g}$ orbitals first before pairing or moving to $e_g$ orbitals. The configuration depends on the number of d-electrons.
For $Cr^{3+}$: Electronic configuration is $3d^3$. In an octahedral field, the configuration is $t_{2g}^3 e_g^0$. Number of unpaired electrons ($n$) = 3.
For $Mn^{3+}$: Electronic configuration is $3d^4$. For low spin, the configuration is $t_{2g}^4 e_g^0$. Number of unpaired electrons ($n$) = 2.
For $Fe^{3+}$: Electronic configuration is $3d^5$. For low spin, the configuration is $t_{2g}^5 e_g^0$. Number of unpaired electrons ($n$) = 1.
For $Co^{3+}$: Electronic configuration is $3d^6$. For low spin, the configuration is $t_{2g}^6 e_g^0$. Number of unpaired electrons ($n$) = 0.
Comparing the number of unpaired electrons:
$Cr^{3+} (3) > Mn^{3+} (2) > Fe^{3+} (1) > Co^{3+} (0)$.
The correct decreasing order is $Cr^{3+} > Mn^{3+} > Fe^{3+} > Co^{3+}$.

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Identify the metal ions among $\mathrm{Co}^{2+}, \mathrm{Ni}^{2+}, \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}, \mathrm{V}^{3+}$ and $\mathrm{Ti}^{2+}$ having a spin-only magnetic moment value more than 3.0 BM. The sum of unpaired electrons present in the high spin octahedral complexes formed by those metal ions is $\_\_\_\_$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **7**

**Detailed Solution:**

The spin-only magnetic moment is given by $\mu = \sqrt{n(n+2)}$ BM, where $n$ is the number of unpaired electrons.
For $\mu > 3.0$ BM, $n$ must be $\ge 3$ (since for $n=2$, $\mu = \sqrt{8} \approx 2.83$ BM and for $n=3$, $\mu = \sqrt{15} \approx 3.87$ BM).
Electronic configurations and unpaired electrons ($n$) in high spin octahedral complexes:
<table><tr><td>Metal Ion</td><td>Configuration</td><td>$n$</td><td>$\mu$ (BM)</td></tr><tr><td>$Co^{2+}$</td><td>$[Ar] 3d^7$</td><td>3</td><td>3.87</td></tr><tr><td>$Ni^{2+}$</td><td>$[Ar] 3d^8$</td><td>2</td><td>2.83</td></tr><tr><td>$Fe^{2+}$</td><td>$[Ar] 3d^6$</td><td>4</td><td>4.90</td></tr><tr><td>$V^{3+}$</td><td>$[Ar] 3d^2$</td><td>2</td><td>2.83</td></tr><tr><td>$Ti^{2+}$</td><td>$[Ar] 3d^2$</td><td>2</td><td>2.83</td></tr></table>
The metal ions with $\mu > 3.0$ BM are $Co^{2+}$ ($n=3$) and $Fe^{2+}$ ($n=4$).
The sum of unpaired electrons for these ions is $3 + 4 = 7$.

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given below are two statements : 
 <b>Statement I</b> : Crystal Field Stabilization Energy (CFSE) of $\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}\right)_{6}\right]^{2+}$ is greater than that of $\left[\mathrm{Mn}\left(\mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}\right)_{6}\right]^{2+}$. 
 <b>Statement II</b>: Potassium ferricyanide has a greater spin-only magnetic moment than sodium ferrocyanide. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\left[\mathrm{Mn}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+} \Rightarrow$ CFSE value is zero because of $\mathrm{d}^5$ configuration with Weak Field Ligands in coordination number 6 $\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}$.

$\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+} \Rightarrow \mathrm{CFSE}$ value is $-0.6 \Delta_0$ because of $\mathrm{d}^4$ configuration with Weak Field Ligands in coordination number 6.

For : $\mathrm{K}_3\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right], \mu=\sqrt{1(1+2)}=\sqrt{3}$ B.M.

For : $\mathrm{Na}_4\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right], \mu=\sqrt{0}$ B.M.

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2025 (4 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

### Q18. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of the complexes $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)\right]^{3+}$ (A),
$\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+}$ (B),
$\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}(\mathrm{C})$ and $\left[\mathrm{CoCl}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5\right]^{2+}(\mathrm{D})$ in terms wavelength of light absorbed is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** D&gt;A&gt;B&gt;C
- **(B)** C&gt;B&gt;D&gt;A
- **(C)** D&gt;C&gt;B&gt;A
- **(D)** C&gt;B&gt;A&gt;D

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) D&gt;A&gt;B&gt;C**

**Detailed Solution:**

We know $\mathrm{E}=\mathrm{h} v=\frac{\mathrm{hC}}{\lambda}$
$\mathrm{E} \propto \frac{1}{\lambda}$
Here all Co in +3 oxidation state.
So, as the ligand field strength $\uparrow, \operatorname{CFSE} \uparrow$
Order of field strength of ligand :
$\mathrm{CN} \gt \mathrm{NH}_3 \gt \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O} \gt \mathrm{Cl}^{-}$
CFSE order : $\mathrm{C} \gt \mathrm{B} \gt \mathrm{A} \gt \mathrm{D}$
Wavelength order: $\mathrm{D} \gt \mathrm{A} \gt \mathrm{B} \gt \mathrm{C}$

</details>

### Q19. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The d-orbital electronic configuration of the complex among $\quad\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{en})_3\right]^{3+}, \quad\left[\mathrm{CoF}_6\right]^{3-}$, $\left[\mathrm{Mn}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Zn}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}$ that has the highest CFSE is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $t_2^6 g_g^0$
- **(B)** $t_2^6 e_g^4$
- **(C)** $t_{2 g}^3 e_g^2$
- **(D)** $t_2^4 e_g^2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $t_2^6 g_g^0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In $\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{en})_3\right]^{+3}$ S.F.L. is present and hence highest value of CFSE
In rest all complexes WFL is present hence CFSE will be low.
$\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{en})_3\right]^{+3} \Rightarrow \mathrm{Co}^{+3}$ and en (SFL)
$\mathrm{Co}^{+3} \Rightarrow[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^6$

### Q21. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements :
Statement (I) : In octahedral complexes, when \(\Delta_{\mathrm{o}}  \lt \mathrm{P}\) high spin complexes are formed. When \(\Delta_{\mathrm{o}}  \gt  \mathrm{P}\) low spin complexes are formed.
Statement (II) : In tetrahedral complexes because of \(\Delta_{\mathrm{t}} \lt \mathrm{P}\), low spin complexes are rarely formed.
In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect.
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect
- **(C)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct**

**Detailed Solution:**

In octahedral complex \((\mathrm{CN}=6)\)
If \(\Delta_0  \lt \) P.E., then high spin complexes are formed
If \(\Delta_0  \gt  \) P.E., then low spin complexes are formed But in tetrahedral complex \((\mathrm{CN}=4)\)
\(\Delta_t \lt \) P.E., then mainly high spin complexes are formed and rarely low spin complexes are formed.

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct increasing order of stability of the complexes based on \(\Delta_{\mathrm{o}}\) value is :
I. \(\left[\mathrm{Mn}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}\)
II. \(\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{4-}\)
III. \(\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{4-}\)
IV. \(\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}\)

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\mathrm{IV} \lt \mathrm{III} \lt \mathrm{II} \lt \mathrm{I}\)
- **(B)** \(\mathrm{I} \lt \mathrm{II} \lt \mathrm{IV} \lt \) III
- **(C)** III \( \lt \) II \( \lt \) IV \( \lt \) I
- **(D)** II \( \lt \) III \( \lt \) I \( \lt \) IV

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) \(\mathrm{I} \lt \mathrm{II} \lt \mathrm{IV} \lt \) III**

**Detailed Solution:**

Neglecting pairing energy
I. $[\mathrm{Mn}(\mathrm{CN}) \mathrm{B}]^{3-} \Rightarrow \mathrm{Mn}^{3+}, \mathrm{t}_2 \mathrm{~g}^4, \mathrm{CFSE}=-0.4 \times 4 \Delta \mathrm{o}$ $=-1.6 \Delta$ 。
II. $[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{CN}) 8]^{4-} \Rightarrow \mathrm{Co}^{2+}, \mathrm{t}_2{ }^6 \mathrm{eg}^1, \mathrm{CFSE}=-0.4 \times 6+$ $0.6 \times 1=-1.8 \Delta_{\circ}$
III. $\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_8\right]^{4-} \Rightarrow \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}, \mathrm{t}_{2 g}{ }^6 \mathrm{e}^0, \mathrm{CFSE}=-0.4 \times 6=$ $-2.4 \Delta_0$
IV. $\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_8\right]^{3-} \Rightarrow \mathrm{Fe}^{3+}, \mathrm{t}_{2 g}{ }^5 \mathrm{e}^0, \mathrm{CFSE}=-0.4 \times 5=$ $-2 \Delta_0$
Order of stability III $>$ IV $>$ II $>$ I

</details>

### Q23. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The metal ion whose electronic configuration is not affected by the nature of the ligand and which gives a violet colour in non-luminous flame under hot condition in borax bead test is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Mn}^{2+}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Ni}^{2+}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Ti}^{3+}$

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{Ni}^{2+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{Ni}^{2+}$ gives violet colour with borax bead test in nonluminous flame under hot conditions. $\mathrm{Ni}^{2+}$ has $\mathrm{d}^8$ configuration which does not depend on nature of ligand present in octahedral field.
$\mathrm{Ni}^{2+} \Rightarrow \mathrm{t}_{2 g}^6 \mathrm{eg}^2$

</details>

### Q24. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The conditions and consequence that favours the $t_{2 g}^3 e_g^1$ configuration in a metal complex are

**Options:**

- **(A)** weak field ligand, low spin complex
- **(B)** weak field ligand, high spin complex
- **(C)** strong field ligand, high spin complex
- **(D)** strong field ligand, low spin complex

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) weak field ligand, high spin complex**

**Detailed Solution:**

The conditions and consequence that favour $t_{2 g}^3 e_g^1$ configuration in a metal complex are
(i) weak field ligand, and
(ii) high spin complex

For weak field ligands, splitting energy ( $\Delta_0$ ) is lower than pairing energy $(P)$. As a result distribution of electrons for $3 \mathrm{~d}^4$ will be $t_{2 \mathrm{~g}}^3 \mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{g}}^1$. It results in high spin complex due to maximum number of unpaired electrons.

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

When Ethane-1,2-diamine is added progressively to an aqueous solution of Nickel (II) chloride, the sequence of colour change observed will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Violet $\rightarrow$ Blue $\rightarrow$ Pale Blue $\rightarrow$ Green
- **(B)** Pale Blue $\rightarrow$ Blue $\rightarrow$ Green $\rightarrow$ Violet
- **(C)** Green $\rightarrow$ Pale Blue $\rightarrow$ Blue $\rightarrow$ Violet
- **(D)** Pale Blue $\rightarrow$ Blue $\rightarrow$ Violet $\rightarrow$ Green

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Green $\rightarrow$ Pale Blue $\rightarrow$ Blue $\rightarrow$ Violet**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\left[\mathrm{Ni}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}+\mathrm{en}_{(\mathrm{aq})} \rightarrow\left[\mathrm{Ni}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_4(\mathrm{en})\right]_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}+2 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$

Green
$\left[\mathrm{Ni}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_4(\mathrm{en})\right]_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}+\mathrm{en}_{(\mathrm{aq})} \rightarrow\left[\mathrm{Ni}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_2(\mathrm{en})_2\right]_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}+2 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$

Pale Blue
$\left[\mathrm{Ni}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_2(\mathrm{en})\right]_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}+\mathrm{en}_{(\mathrm{aq})} \rightarrow\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{en})_3\right]_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}+2 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$

Blue / purple

</details>

### Q27. JEE Main 2025 (23 Jan Shift 1)

- **(B)** $\mathrm{t}_{2 \mathrm{~g}}{ }^6 \mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{g}}{ }^1$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{t}_{2 \mathrm{~g}}{ }^5 \mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{g}}{ }^2$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{e}^4 \mathrm{t}_2{ }^3$

### Q28. JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The correct order of the following complexes in terms of their crystal field stabilization energies is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4\right]^{2+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{2+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{en})_3\right]^{3+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+}$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{2+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4\right]^{2+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{en})_3\right]^{3+}$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{en})_3\right]^{3+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{2+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4\right]^{2+}$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4\right]^{2+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{2+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{en})_3\right]^{3+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4\right]^{2+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{2+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+} \lt \left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{en})_3\right]^{3+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Crystal field splitting energy $(\Delta) \propto$ charge or oxidation state of central metal atom.

Crystal field splitting energy $(\Delta) \propto$ Field strength of ligand (and chelation)
Crystal field stabilisation energy (CFSE) $=\left[-0.4 \mathrm{t}_{\mathrm{g}_g}\right.$ +0.6 eg] $\Delta_{\circ}$ (for octahedral)
For,
$\begin{aligned} & {\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{en})_3\right]^{3+}: \mathrm{Co}^{3+}: \mathrm{t}_{2 \mathrm{~g}}^6 \mathrm{eg}^0 ; \mathrm{CFSE}=-2.4\left(\Delta_0\right)_1} \\ & {\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+}: \mathrm{Co}^{3+}: \mathrm{t}_{2 \mathrm{~g}}^6 \mathrm{eg}^0 ; \mathrm{CFSE}=-2.4\left(\Delta_0\right)_2} \\ & {\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{2+}: \mathrm{Co}^{2+}: \mathrm{t}_{2 g}^5 \mathrm{eg}^2 ; \mathrm{CFSE}=-0.8\left(\Delta_0\right)_3} \\ & {\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4\right]^{2+}: \mathrm{Co}^{2+}: \mathrm{e}^4 \mathrm{t}_2^3 ; \mathrm{CFSE}=-1.2 \Delta_{\mathrm{t}}} \\ & \left(\mathrm{as}:\left(\Delta_{\mathrm{t}}\right)=\frac{4}{9}\left(\Delta_0\right)_3\right) \\ & \because \quad \Delta_{\mathrm{t}} < \left(\Delta_0\right)_3 < \left(\Delta_0\right)_2 < \left(\Delta_0\right)_1\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q32. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

he following complexes

**Options:**

- **(A)** C \(  < \) D \( < \) A \( < \) B
- **(B)** \(\mathrm{B}  < \mathrm{C} < \mathrm{A} < \mathrm{D}\)
- **(C)** A \(  < \) C \( < \) B \( < \) D
- **(D)** D \(  < \) A \( < \) C \( < \) B

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) D \(  < \) A \( < \) C \( < \) B**

**Detailed Solution:**

As ligand field increases, light of more energy is absorbed
Energy $\propto$ wave number
$(\bar{v})$

</details>

### Q33. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The number of complexes from the following with no electrons in the \(t_2\) orbital is \(\qquad\) \(\mathrm{TiCl}_4,\left[\mathrm{MnO}_4\right]^{-},\left[\mathrm{FeO}_4\right]^{2-},\left[\mathrm{FeCl}_4\right]^{-},\left[\mathrm{CoCl}_4\right]^{2-}\)

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** 4
- **(C)** 3
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 3**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{ll}\mathrm{TiCl}_4 \Rightarrow \mathrm{Ti}^{+4} & \mathrm{e}^0 \mathrm{t}_2^0 \\ \mathrm{MnO}_4^{-} \Rightarrow \mathrm{Mn}^{+7} & \mathrm{e}^0 \mathrm{t}_2^0 \\ \mathrm{FeO}_4^{2-} \Rightarrow \mathrm{Fe}^{+6} & \mathrm{e}^2 \mathrm{t}_2^0 \\ \mathrm{FeCl}_4^{2-} \Rightarrow \mathrm{Fe}^{+2} & \mathrm{e}^3 \mathrm{t}_2^3 \\ \mathrm{CoCl}_4^{2-} \Rightarrow \mathrm{Co}^{+2} & \mathrm{e}^4 \mathrm{t}_2^3\end{array}$

</details>

---

### Q34. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

If an iron (III) complex with the formula \(\left[\mathrm{Fe}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_x(\mathrm{CN})_y\right]^{-}\)has no electron in its \(e_g\) orbital, then the value of \(x+y\) is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 4
- **(B)** 5
- **(C)** 6
- **(D)** 3

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 6**

**Detailed Solution:**

Complex is<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/YgJnywZRMafOYF4pUcpPgPIEg93LjbMcp-0uA9JaSmk.original.fullsize.png">
$\begin{aligned} & x=2 \\ & y=4 \\ & \text { so } x+y=6\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q35. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> are respectively known as:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Spin free Complex, Spin paired Complex
- **(B)** Spin paired Complex, Spin free Complex
- **(C)** Outer orbital Complex, Inner orbital Complex
- **(D)** Inner orbital Complex, Spin paired Complex

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Spin paired Complex, Spin free Complex**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In the complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> Ammonia is strong field ligand and electronic configuration of </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Hybridisation: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></p><p>Pairing of electrons takes place due to the strong field ligand. Hence, it is inner orbital complex (spin paired complex).</p><p>In the complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> fluoride is weak field ligand and electronic configuration of  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow></mfenced></math>.</p><p>Hybridisation: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>Pairing of electrons not takes place due to the weak field ligand. Hence, it is outer orbital complex (spin free complex).</p><p>Hence option B is the answer.</p>

</details>

---

### Q36. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Given below are two statements:
Statement (I): A solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is green in colour.
Statement (II): A solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is colourless.
In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect
- **(B)** <p>Both Statement I and Statement II are correct</p>
- **(C)** <p>Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct</p>
- **(D)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <p>Both Statement I and Statement II are correct</p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> is a weak field ligand. Therefore, there are unpaired electrons in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>. In this complex, the <i>d</i> electrons from the lower energy level can be excited to the higher energy level i.e., the possibility of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math>transition is present. Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is coloured.</p><p>In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>, the electrons are all paired as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> is a strong field ligand. Therefore, <i><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> </i>transition is not possible in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>. Hence, it is colourless.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo></math>Green colour solution due to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> transition.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo></math>is diamagnetic and it is colourless.</p>

</details>

<td>I.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math></td> open="["><mrow><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td> <td>II.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td>C.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td> <td>III.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td>D.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>V</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td> <td>IV.</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math></td> </tr> </tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-III, B-II, C-IV, D-I
- **(B)** A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III
- **(C)** A-IV, B-III, C-I, D-II
- **(D)** A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></math> (triplet, lower energy) orbitals are the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>xy</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>xz</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>yz</mi></msub></math> orbitals. The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub></math> (doublet, higher energy) orbitals are the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></msub></math> orbitals. These split d-orbitals are called <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub></math>because they are split by the effect of the surrounding ligand ions in a crystal field.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> Contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>:</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> Contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>:</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> Contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>:</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> Contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>:</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math></p>

</details>

- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>OH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>number</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi ### Q39. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements, one is labelled as assertion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">A</mi></math> and the other is labelled as Reason <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">R</mi></math>.
assertion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">A</mi></math> : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>CoCl</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> absorbs at lower wavelength of light with respect to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
Reason <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">R</mi></math>: It is because the wavelenght of light absorbed depends on the oxidation state of the metal ion.
In the light of the above statements,choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">A</mi></math> is false but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">R</mi></math> is true
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">A</mi></math> is true but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">R</mi></math> is false
- **(C)** Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">R</mi></math> are true and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">R</mi></math> is the correct explaination of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">A</mi></math>
- **(D)** Both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">R</mi></math> are true and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">R</mi></math> is NOT the correct explaination of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">A</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">A</mi></math> is false but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="bold">R</mi></math> is true**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>CoCl</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>absorbs with higher wavelength of light with respect to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> because <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> is weaker ligand than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>. The crystal field stabilisation is higher for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>, hence we can say that wavelength of light absorbed depends upon the oxidation state of the metal ion.

</details>

### Q40. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Match List I with List II<table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align: center;"><strong>LIST-I
			Complex</strong></td>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align: center;"><strong>LIST-II
			Colour</strong></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;">A.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mg</mi><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">I.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;">brown</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;">B.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">II.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;">white</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;">C.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MnO</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>OH</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">III.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;">yellow</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;">D.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">IV.</td>			<td style="text-align: center;">blue</td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-III, B-IV, C-II, D-I
- **(B)** A-II, B-III, C-I, D-IV
- **(C)** A-II, B-IV, C-I, D-III
- **(D)** A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A-II, B-III, C-I, D-IV**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The formula <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mg</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>)</mo><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> represents magnesium ammonium phosphate it is white in colour. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> is known as potassium hexanitritocobaltate(III). It is yellow in colour. The yellow colour is due to the presence of the cobalt ion (Co3+) in the complex, which absorbs certain wavelengths of light and reflects yellow light.  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MnO</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>OH</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math>is brown in colour. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is blue in colour. </p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;">Complex</td>			<td style="text-align: center;">Colour</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mg</mi><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">White </td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">Yellow</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MnO</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>OH</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">Brown</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">Blue</td>		</tr>	</tbody></table>

</details>

This splitting is due to the presence of ligands. ### Q42. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In potassium ferrocyanide, there are ______ pairs of electrons in the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></math> set of orbitals.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Potassium ferrocyanide, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>, has a central iron ion that is coordinated with six cyanide ligands in an octahedral geometry. The oxidation state of the iron ion in potassium ferrocyanide is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, hence has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> hybridised state. On applying <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>. it shows that all the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn></math> electrons of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> subshell are present in the form of three pairs in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></math> orbitals. Hence answer is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>.</p><p><img height="430" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fccdc97d-ba32-4a95-8e17-7746bc4a3da7.png" width="582" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q43. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements: one is labelled as &ldquo;Assertion A&rdquo; and the other is labelled as &ldquo;Reason R&rdquo;</p><p>Assertion A : In the complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>5</mn></msub></math>, the metals have zero oxidation state.</p><p>Reason R : Low oxidation states are found when a complex has ligands capable of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#960;</mi></math>-donor character in addition to the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#963;</mi></math>-bonding.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A is correct but R is not correct
- **(B)** A is not correct but R is correct
- **(C)** Both A and R are correct but R is NOT the correct explanation of A
- **(D)** Both A and R are correct and R is the correct explanation of A

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) A is correct but R is not correct**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The ligand carbon monoxide has no charge, hence metals have zero oxidation state in both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>5</mn></msub></math>. Low oxidation states are found when a complex has ligands capable of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#960;</mi></math>-acceptor character in addition to the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#963;</mi></math>-bonding.</p><p>In the complexes, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub></math>, the ligand <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>-</mo></math>acceptor character in addition to the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>bonding</mi></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q44. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Which of the following complex will show largest splitting of d-orbitals?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Some ligands are able to produce strong fields in which case, the splitting will be large whereas others produce weak fields and consequently result in a small splitting of d orbitals.</p><p>In general, ligands can be arranged in a series called spectrochemical series according to which maximum splitting is done by the strongest ligand which is cyanide (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>) ion in this case.</p><p> </p>

</details>

---

### Q45. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>If the CFSE of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ti</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>, this complex will absorb maximum at wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>nm</mi></math>. (nearest integer)</p><p>Assume Planck&#39; constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mi>Js</mi></math>, Speed of light <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> and Avogadro&#39;s constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>23</mn></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>.</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **480**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ti</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>, oxidation state of Titanium is +3.
Thus, electronic configuration:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ti</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>We know CFSE <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#916;o</mi></math>       (      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;o</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Octahedral</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>splitting</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>energy</mi></math>)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;o</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>96</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>23</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>Using planck&#39;s equation and fact that octahedral splitting energy per complex is:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#916;o</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>480</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>480</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math></p><p> </p>

</details>
