### Q46. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Correct order of spin only magnetic moment of the following complex ions is:</p><p>(Given At. No. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>26</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Co</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>27</mn></math>)</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(1) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bb149bc2-09bb-428d-9819-a1c4a3b680dd-9895301.png" style="width: 400px; height: 187px;" /></p><p>Number of unpaired <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>35</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p>(2) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/48dbc2ad-0943-4199-8cac-e38de525e7ac-9895301_2.png" style="width: 400px; height: 182px;" /></p><p>Number of unpaired <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>24</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p>(3) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a70f5e3b-9746-4128-b028-e807ca7e08db-9895301_3.png" style="width: 400px; height: 169px;" /></p><p>Number of unpaired <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p>So, the correct order is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q47. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Match List I with List II</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td> </td>			<td>List I
			Coordination entity</td>			<td> </td>			<td>List II
			Wavelength of light absorbed in nm</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>A</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>CoCl</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>I.</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>310</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>B</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>II.</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>475</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>III.</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>535</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>D</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>IV.</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below :-</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Co-ordination compounds absorb a particular wavelength following certain rules.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>Wavelength&#160;of&#160;light&#160;absorbed&#160;</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8733;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mtext>Oxidation&#160;state&#160;of&#160;metal&#160;ion</mtext></mfrac></math></p><p>                                                              <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8733;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mtext>Strength&#160;of&#160;ligand</mtext></mfrac></math></p><p>Ligand field strength - <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td> </td>			<td>List I
			Coordination entity</td>			<td> </td>			<td>List II
			Wavelength of light absorbed in nm</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>A</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>CoCl</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>I.</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>535</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>B</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>II.</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>475</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>III.</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>310</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>D</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>IV.</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p> </p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q48. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following cannot be explained by crystal field theory?

**Options:**

- **(A)** The order of spectrochemical series
- **(B)** Magnetic properties of transition metal complexes
- **(C)** Colour of metal complexes
- **(D)** Stability of metal complexes

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) The order of spectrochemical series**

**Detailed Solution:**

The crystal field model is successful in explaining the formation, structures, colour and magnetic properties of coordination compounds to a large extent. However, from the assumptions that the ligands are point charges, it follows that anionic ligands should exert the greatest splitting effect. The anionic ligands actually are found at the low end of the spectrochemical series. So, it couldn&#39;t explain successfully the order of spectrochemical series.

</details>

---

### Q49. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> should be an inner orbital complex. Ignoring the pairing energy, the value of crystal field stabilization energy for this complex is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mo>-</mo></mfenced></math>_____<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> In this the central metal Fe is in +3 oxidation state. And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> is strong field ligand so, it will form inner orbital complex.</p><p>Electronic configuration in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math></p><p>Electronic configuration for inner orbital complex: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/97c81f55-2d0e-42a9-b74f-0724858d6b9b-image.png" style="width: 262px; height: 100px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CFSE</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p>So, final answer will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q50. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The spin only magnetic moment of the complex present in Fehling&#39;s reagent is____<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math> (Round off your answer to the nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Fehling&#39;s solution consists of two parts A and B.  Fehling&rsquo;s solution A  is deep blue colour due to the bis(tartrate) complex of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>. Fehling&#39;s solution B is the Rochelle salt (potassium sodium tartrate tetrahydrate) serves as a chelating agent in the solution.</p><p>Fehling solution is a complex of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>9</mn></msup></math></p><p>Number of unpaired <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Magnetic</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Moment</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mi>BM</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The correct order of energy of absorption for the following metal complexes is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>:</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>en</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>:</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>:</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Stronger the ligand, larger the splitting energy &amp; higher the energy of absorption. Among the given options en is the strongest ligand due to ring formation than ammonia and than water. In all complex metal ion is same with same oxidation state. </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><munder><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>en</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></munder><mo>&#62;</mo><munder><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></munder><mo>&#62;</mo><munder><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></munder></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q52. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Consider the following metal complexes :</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>CoCl</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>The spin-only magnetic moment value of the complex that absorbs light with shortest wavelength is B.M. (Nearest integer)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **0**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The complex that absorbs minimum wave length of light has maximum splitting</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>Crystal</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>field</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>stabilization</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>energy</mi></math></p><p>Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> being <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>strong</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>field</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ligand</mi></math> ,It will have cause CFSE So,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> will absorb minimum wavelength.</p><p>The hybridization of Co in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace></math></p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f99a48b7-7811-4bbb-83a2-8c9fa15d1d0e-8-8-2022new.png" style="width: 500px; height: 157px;" />As, the complex has no unpaired electrons, spin only  magnetic moment will be zero and it is diamagnetic in nature.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q53. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements :</p><p>Statement I: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is square planar and diamagnetic complex, with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridization for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi></math> but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> is tetrahedral, paramagnetic and with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> hybridication for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi></math>.</p><p>Statement II : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> both have same d-electron configuration, have same geometry and are paramagnetic.</p><p>In light the above statements, choose the correct answer form the options given below</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true.
- **(B)** Statement I is correct but statement II is false.
- **(C)** Statement I is incorrect but statement II is true.
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Both Statement I and Statement II are false.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup></math> configuration, SFL, sq. planar splitting <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfenced></math>, diamagnetic.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>10</mn></msup></math> config (after excitation), SFL, tetrahedral splitting <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mfenced></math>, diamagnetic.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup></math> config, WFL, tetrahedral splitting <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mfenced></math>, paramagnetic(<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> unpaired <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>).</p><p>Electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi></math> atom in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></math></p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/52743590-672b-4f6f-809d-fc2fc2d4177b-b886ed1d-ef76-4542-8c3f-cbe0371f1f83-9709720.png" style="width: 550px; height: 568px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Reaction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> with excess ammonia and in the presence of oxygen results into a diamagnetic product. Number of electrons present in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></math>-orbitals of the product is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>air</mi></mover><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup></math></p><p>Electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math></p><p>Air oxides the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Co</mi></math>(II) to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Co</mi></math>(III)</p>

</details>

### Q55. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> absorbs a light of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> transition, then the value of octahedral crystal field splitting energy for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> will be____<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>21</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> [Nearest integer]</p><p>(Given : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>63</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mi>Js</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>08</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **766**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>63</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>08</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>600</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>340</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>34</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>21</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>340</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>34</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>21</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>765</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>765</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>21</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>766</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>21</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>Note: According to official answer key provided by NTA answer is 745. But according to calculations answer is = 766.</p>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Transition metal complex with highest value of crystal field splitting <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfenced></math> will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Mo</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Os</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Os</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> is a weak field ligand. Crystal field stabilisation energy is maximum for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup></math> cases with weak field ligands among the 3d-series elements.But <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CFSE</mi></math> increases from 3d to 5d series elements due to the increase in nuclear charge. Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Os</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> complex case, the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CFSE</mi></math> is maximum. </p><p> </p>

</details>

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The Crystal Field Stabilization Energy (CFSE) and magnetic moment (spin-only) of an octahedral aqua complex of a metal ion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mi>Z</mi><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>87</mn></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>BM</mi></math>, respectively. Identify <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mi>Z</mi><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced><mo>:</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

<p>CFSE for octahedral complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>eg</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math ### Q58. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Acidic ferric chloride solution on treatment with excess of potassium ferrocyanide gives a Prussian blue coloured colloidal species. It is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HFe</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mo>Fe</mo><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KFe</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KFe</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>If we add ferric chloride over potassium ferrocyanide, it forms a white precipitate that will become blue. The reaction is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>FeCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#10230;</mo><mi>KFe</mi><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>KCl</mi></math></p><p>Being small quantity of ferric chloride, not all potassium is displaced.</p>

</details>

### Q59. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Arrange the following Cobalt complexes in the order of increasing Crystal Field Stabilization Energy (CFSE) value.</p><p>Complexes: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><munder><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></munder><mo>,</mo></mtd><mtd><munder><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></munder><mo>,</mo></mtd><mtd><munder><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></munder><mtext>&#160;and&#160;</mtext><munder><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>en</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></munder></mtd><mtd><mspace/></mtd></mtr></mtable><mspace linebreak="newline"/></math></p><p>Choose the correct option :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>CFSE <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8733;</mo></math> Strength of ligand</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>en</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p>CFSE <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8733;</mo></math> Oxidation number of the central metal.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Order of CFSE</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>en</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which one of the following complexes is violet in colour?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>NOS</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>SCN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>NOS</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Sulphur of organic compounds is detected by using sodium nitroprusside. With it sulphur forms a violet colour complex, called sodium thionitroprusside.<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>5</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi>NO</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>Sodium</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nitroprusside</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>5</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi>NOS</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>Violet</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>colour</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q61. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements :
Statement I: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> hybridised.
Statement II : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>MnCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> are paramagnetic and have <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math> unpaired electrons, respectively. </p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is correct but statement II is false
- **(B)** Both statement I and statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is incorrect but statement II is true
- **(D)** Both statement I and statement II are are true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Both statement I and statement II are are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img height="111" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/85e3968a-f3ac-463b-b216-4c3dd5e93f6b-image.png" width="359" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> is strong field ligand. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> is chelating ligand. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></math> configuration. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math> configuration. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math> configuration.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> All will have larger splitting values hence, they undergo <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> hybridisation.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>MnCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>                              <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></math> configuration, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>             <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math> configuration, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/31cbe4c7-d5c8-4597-80a3-25ec6b7bc001-image.png" style="width: 304px; height: 198px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q62. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The type of hybridisation and magnetic property of the complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>MnCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> respectively, are:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> and diamagnetic
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and diamagnetic
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and paramagnetic
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> and paramagnetic

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> and paramagnetic**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>MnCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msup></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8b4882a1-06fb-4346-a130-59d4ba4bfc4e-image.png" style="width: 375px; height: 107px;" /></p><p>Paramagnetic and having <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> unpaired electrons.</p>

</details>

### Q63. JEE Main 2021 (22 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The total number of unpaired electrons present in $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_{3}\right)_{6}\right] \mathrm{Cl}_{2}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_{3}\right)_{6}\right] \mathrm{Cl}_{3}$ is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>7</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math>
For strong field ligand and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>7</mn></msup></math> configuration: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></msubsup></math> 
Total <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> unpaired electron is present.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math>
The hybridisation of the complex is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> hybridisation.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> acts as strong field ligand, because <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>.</mo></math>.
So here all electrons becomes paired.</p>

</details>

### Q64. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

What is the spin-only magnetic moment value (B.M.) of a divalent metal ion with atomic number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>,</mo></math> in its aqueous solution?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** zero
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>26</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Electronic configuration of divalent metal ion having atomic number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn></math> is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bdbb56db-49f7-4af7-9601-387b41f43d96-image.png" style="width: 400px; height: 52px;" /></p><p>Total number of unpaired electrons<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></math> (Magnetic moment) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p>where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo></math> number of unpaired <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>35</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q65. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ti</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> absorbs light of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>498</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> during a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> transition. The octahedral splitting energy for the above complex is _______ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. (Round off to the Nearest Integer). <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>626</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Js</mi><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>absorbed</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>498</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> (given)</p><p>The octahedral splitting energy </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0399</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>17</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>99</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>   (round off)</p>

</details>

---

### Q66. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The spin only magnetic moment of a divalent ion in aqueous solution (atomic number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>29</mn></math>) is ___ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>BM</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>29</mn><mfenced><mi>Cu</mi></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> form <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> complex ion with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>9</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>WFL</mi></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7002bec0-6e26-4f97-8a2d-0da01c05736d-image.png" style="width: 192px; height: 54px;" /></p><p>number of unpaired <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo></math>round off ans. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q67. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>In which of the following order the given complex ions are arranged correctly with respect to their decreasing spin only magnetic moment?
(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>(iii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>(iv) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Complex
(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> is WFL</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5bc8ff5d-5777-4c7e-9027-bb5308217b70-image.png" style="width: 184px; height: 57px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>35</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math></p><p>(ii)<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is SFL</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/26cd2ef6-9276-4544-8286-035c39288b9e-image.png" style="width: 196px; height: 60px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math></p><p>(iii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>WFL</mi></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/58772098-175d-47cc-9943-514609615543-image.png" style="width: 184px; height: 52px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>8</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math></p><p>(iv) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>9</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>SFL</mi></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/15b142b2-4c83-493d-84af-5f7f8c4c7414-image.png" style="width: 336px; height: 57px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math></p><p>Thus correct order of spin only magnetic moment is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iv</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q68. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The hybridization and magnetic nature of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> respectively are :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> and diamagnetic
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and paramagnetic
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> and paramagnetic
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and diamagnetic

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and paramagnetic**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Magnetic property: Due to presence of one unpaired electrons in 3d shell, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo></mrow></msup></math>  ion is paramagnetic in nature.</p><p>â€‹â€‹â€‹â€‹<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0b39fbcb-474b-4e66-b490-861494be8392-image.png" style="width: 281px; height: 120px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> hybridisation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>
and due to presence of unpaired <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> complex is paramagnetic in nature</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/78bb0178-2057-4f7d-8937-f40df3182fd1-ff8fe2f9-cfa7-49e2-b318-707a0b81bc1b-image.png" style="width: 289px; height: 120px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> hybridisation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>
and due to presence of unpaired <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> complex paramagnetic in nature</p>

</details>
