### Q69. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The calculated magnetic moments (spin only value) for species <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>MnO</mi><mn>4</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> respectively are:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>83</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>82</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>90</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/617d9031-049a-411c-acd4-d090bc2c1fed-2c1be671-5e2a-4a20-833d-38eadec38035-image.png" style="width: 318px; height: 127px;" /></p><p>(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>4</mn></math> unpaired electrons.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>24</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p> </p><p>(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> is a strong field ligand &amp; causes
pairing &amp; hence no. unpaired electron</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d1612045-52d8-467e-a0ad-f00d7e398cfc-bdae788c-fb0c-4415-bbc3-8d5935175097-image.png" style="width: 247px; height: 134px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p> </p><p>(iii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>MnO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></math> it has one unpaired electron.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>BM</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q70. JEE Main 2020 (06 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

For a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></math> metal ion in an octahedral field, the correct electronic configuration is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><msub><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mn>3</mn></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>1</mn></msubsup></math> when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><msub><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mn>3</mn></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>1</mn></msubsup></math> when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><msub><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mn>4</mn></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math> when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><msub><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math> when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><msub><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mn>3</mn></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>1</mn></msubsup></math> when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></math> configuration if <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> the electronic configuration is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>1</mn></msubsup></math>.

</details>

### Q75. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The Crystal Field Stabilization Energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>CFSE</mi><mo>)</mo></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>CoF</mi><mmultiscripts><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn><none></none><mprescripts></mprescripts><mn>3</mn><none></none></mmultiscripts></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mn>9</mn><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CFSE</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>nt</mi><mn>29</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>og</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>]</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mn>0</mn></msub></math> </p>

</details>

### Q76. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The d-electron configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ru</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>en</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>, and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> respectively are:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Ru</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>en</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Ru</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q77. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The electronic spectrum of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ti</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> shows a single broad peak with a maximum at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>300</mn><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math>The crystal field stabilization energy (CFSE) of the complex ion, in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>kJmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> is : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>kJmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>83</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** 145.5
- **(B)** 242.5
- **(C)** 83.7
- **(D)** 97

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 97**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ti</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>Ti</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>CFSE <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>eg</mi></msub><mo>]</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>]</mo><mn>20300</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8120</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>83</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mole</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mole</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q78. JEE Main 2020 (02 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>For octahedral <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math> and tetrahedral <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math> complexes, consider the following statements :</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo></math> both the complexes can be high spin.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>)</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math> complex can very rarely be of low spin.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>)</mo></math> with strong field ligands, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math> complexes can be low spin.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>)</mo></math> aqueous solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ions</mi></math> is yellow in color.</p><p>The correct statements are :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>)</mo></math> only
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced></math> only
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced></math> only
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced></math> only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced></math> only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>With weak field ligands Mn(II) will be of high spin and with strong field ligands it will be of low spin. Ni(II) tetrahedral complexes will be genrally of high spin due to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> hybridisation. It can very rarely be of low spin when ligand field is very strong.</p><p>Mn(II) is of light pink color in aqueous solution.</p>

</details>

---

### Q79. JEE Main 2020 (02 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math> metal ion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced></math> forms a complex with aqua ligands, and the spin only magnetic moment of the complex is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.90</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>BM</mi></math>. The geometry and the crystal field stabilization energy of the complex is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>&#160;octahedral and&#160;</mtext><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>P</mi></math>
- **(B)** tetrahedral and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.6</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>&#160;octahedral and&#160;</mtext><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>&#160;tetrahedral and&#160;</mtext><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>P</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) tetrahedral and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.6</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Since spin only magnetic moment is 4.90 BM so number of unpaired electrons must be 4. There are two possibilities:</p><p>(1) If the complex is octahedral, then it has to be high spin complex with configuration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo></math> in that case <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CFSE</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
(2) If the complex is tetrahedral then its electronic configuration will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi></msub><msup><mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><none></none><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mmultiscripts><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CFSE</mi></math> will <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>be</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>X</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q80. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Complete removal of both the axial ligands (along the <math><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> ) from an octahedral complex leads to which of the following splitting patterns? (relative orbital energies not on scale).

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9672328a-b57e-4543-ab61-4fe7fdbbdd4f-image12.png">
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3f2c397b-0515-4eb1-a490-3f641debaa4e-image13.png">
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4c57923c-1ba0-4675-b411-7a70ecfface5-image14.png">
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/22cba690-fd54-4504-8197-82db0f99ea39-image15.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/22cba690-fd54-4504-8197-82db0f99ea39-image15.png">**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c3fb195a-fa97-454b-bdc4-41b668848bd3-image16.png" /></p><p>When crystal field splitting energy is small, the pairing energy exceeds the splitting energy, and the electrons will fill the d orbitals as if they were degenerate; this is classified as high spin.</p><p>We find that the square planar complexes have the greatest crystal field splitting energy compared to all the other complexes. This means that most square planar complexes are low spin, strong field ligands.</p>

</details>

---

### Q81. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The complex ion that will lose its crystal field stabilization energy upon oxidation of metal to <math><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math> state is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fb6b204a-a2d3-4385-aabc-66b082fe8260-image.png" style="width: 265px; height: 164px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Zn</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><msup><mrow><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><munder><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></munder><mo>&rarr;</mo><munder><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></munder></math>
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/46699862-be6f-4596-9b72-f2285f21ed2a-image.png" style="width: 540px; height: 154px;" />
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&times;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>0.4</mn></mrow></mfenced><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&Delta;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2.4</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&Delta;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2.0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&Delta;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math></p><p>In all other cases, the electron is removed from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub></math> orbital and is hence, this will lead to decrease in pairing energy.</p>

</details>

### Q82. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The crystal field stabilization energy (CFSE) of <math><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>, respectively, are:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>0.4</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></math> and  <math><mo>-</mo><mn>1.2</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>2.4</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>  and  <math><mo>-</mo><mn>1.2</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>0.4</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><mo>-</mo><mn>0.8</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>0.6</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>  and  <math><mo>-</mo><mn>0.8</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mo>-</mo><mn>0.4</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><mo>-</mo><mn>0.8</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&rArr;</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math><math><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></math> Since <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> is

Weak field ligands,
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bd575639-93a7-49b7-be13-a8c8694ad78d-image.png" style="width: 502px; height: 214px;" />  
CFSE <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mn>4</mn></mfenced><mo>&times;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>0.4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>0.6</mn><mo>&times;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0.4</mn><msub><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">0</mi></msub></math>
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/443acc75-d326-497b-aa6b-c7e2fedd87b1-image.png" style="width: 476px; height: 196px;" /> CFSE <math><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&times;</mo><mn>0.4</mn><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0.6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&times;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0.8</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><mo>-</mo><mn>0.8</mn><msub><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Three complexes,
<math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
absorb light in the visible region. The correct order of the wavelength of light absorbed by them is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

On increasing strength of ligand, wavelength of absorbed radiation decreases 
Strength of ligands according to spectrochemical series is: <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>  
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></math> Order of wavelength absorbed will be <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></math>  
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></math> Correct order of wavelength absorbed is 
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>

</details>

### Q85. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The difference in the number of unpaired electrons of a metal ion in its high-spin and low-spin octahedral complexes is two. The metal ion is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The electronic configuration for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>27</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>7</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math>

When it combines with weak ligands (high spin complex),<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,2</mo><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>eg</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,1</mo></mrow></msup></math>, it has <math><mn>3</mn></math> unpaired electrons, whereas when it bonds with strong ligands (low spin complex), <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,2</mo><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>eg</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,0</mo></mrow></msup></math>, then, it has <math><mn>1</mn></math> unpaired electron.</p><p>The difference in number of unpaired electrons is equal to two.</p>

</details>

### Q86. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The complex that has highest crystal field splitting energy <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo></mrow></mfenced></math> , is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi> </mi></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>o</mi><msub><mrow><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mi>C</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi> </mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi> </mi></mrow></msup><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mi> </mi></mrow><mrow><mi> </mi></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><msup><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi> </mi></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Strong field ligands have more <math><mi>C</mi><mi>F</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>E</mi></math> value while weak field ligand have less <math><mi>C</mi><mi>F</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>E</mi></math> value.

<math><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is strongest ligand among all so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></msup></math> has maximum <math><mi>C</mi><mi>F</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>E</mi></math> value.

</details>

### Q87. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Two complexes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced></math>  are violet and yellow coloured, respectively, The incorrect statement regarding them is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both are paramagnetic with three unpaired electrons
- **(B)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>  value of  <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>  is less than that of  <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> values of <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> are calculated from the energies violet and yellow light, respectively.
- **(D)** Both absorb energies corresponding to their complementary colors.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> values of <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> are calculated from the energies violet and yellow light, respectively.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub></math> , both complex have <math><mi>C</mi><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>,</mo></math> B has strong field ligand and A has weak field ligand

so <math><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mtext>&#160;</mtext><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub></math>

In both complexes, <math><mi>C</mi><mi>r</mi></math> has <math><mn>3</mn></math> unpaired electrons and hence, will be paramagnetic <math><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>r</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> in strong field ligand or weak field ligand is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo><mi>e</mi><msup><mi>g</mi><mi>o</mi></msup></math>

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e2d4dc5c-42f8-4b4a-bf85-a05ca305481f-image.png" style="width: 350px; height: 121px;" />

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> order will be compared by spectrochemical series not by energies of violet and yellow light. Hence incorrect</p>

</details>

### Q88. JEE Main 2016 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Identify the correct trend given below:

(Atomic No. = Ti : 22, Cr : 24 and Mo : 42)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><mrow><mo>∆</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

As the oxidation state of metal increases the CFSE value increases hence  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mo>&#8201;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi>Ti</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi>Ti</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

 <math> <mrow> <mtext>&#916;</mtext> </mrow> </math> increase from 3d to 4d Series. So,<sub> </sub>  <p><math> <mrow> <mtext>&#916;</mtext> <mo>&#8733;</mo> </mrow> </math> CFSE (Crystal field stabilisation energy)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>of</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Mo</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Because here <math> <mrow> <mtext>&#916;</mtext> </mrow> </math> depends on Z<sub>eff</sub> &amp; Z<sub>eff</sub> of 4d series is more than 3d series  </p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2015 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

When concentrated <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math> is added to aqueous solution of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>, its colour changes from reddish pink to deep blue. Which complex ion gives blue colour in reaction?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

On addition of conc. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math>. The equilibrium between two coloured Co (II) species can be described by equation

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><msubsup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mtext>Co</mtext><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mtext>H</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mtext>O</mtext></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mtext>aq</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></mtd><mtd><mo>+</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>4</mn><msubsup><mtext>Cl</mtext><mtext>aq</mtext><mo>-</mo></msubsup></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8652;</mo></mtd><mtd><msubsup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mtext>CoCl</mtext><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mtext>aq</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></mtd><mtd><mo>+</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>6</mn><msub><mtext>H</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mtext>O</mtext><mfenced><mi>&#8467;</mi></mfenced></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mfenced><mtext>Pink</mtext></mfenced></mtd><mtd></mtd><mtd></mtd><mtd></mtd><mtd><mfenced><mtext>Blue</mtext></mfenced></mtd><mtd></mtd><mtd></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>

The equilibrium is disturbed by changing the conc. of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> ions by addition of conc. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math> in forward direction.

</details>

### Q90. JEE Main 2015 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following complex ions has electrons that are symmetrically filled in both <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></math> orbitals?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Metal ion having either <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>or</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>10</mn></msup></math> configuration can be filled symmetrically in both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub></math> orbitals. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>7</mn></msup></math> configuration, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math> configuration. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math> configuration but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> is strong field ligand and it will pair the electrons due to which electronic configuration is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mn>5</mn></msup><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mn>0</mn></msup></math>.</p><p><img alt="" height="127" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/image21-0d1b3aa4-287e-470d-af50-f53f3b0bc373.jpg" width="406" /></p><p>As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> is weak field ligand hence back pairing of electrons do not take place or in case of weak field ligand pairing energy of electrons (P )is greater than crystal field splitting energy in octahedral complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>o</mi></msub></mfenced></math> i.e.<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>o</mi></msub></math> , hence electrons will go in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi></msub></math> orbitals, instead of back paired in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub></math> orbitals.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q91. JEE Main 2014 (19 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Amongst the following, identify the species with an atom in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></math> oxidation state.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>CrO</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>Cl</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>Cr</mtext> <msub> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>CN</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>6</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>-</mo> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>Cr</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>O</mtext> <mn>3</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>MnO</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mo>-</mo> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>CrO</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>Cl</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mover> <mtext>Cr</mtext> <mrow> <mo>+</mo> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> </mover> <mover> <mtext>O</mtext> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mover> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msub> <mrow> <mover> <mtext>Cl</mtext> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mover> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>

<math> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mover> <mtext>Cr</mtext> <mrow> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mover> <msub> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>CN</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>6</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mover> <mtext>Cr</mtext> <mrow> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mover> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msub> <mrow> <mover> <mtext>O</mtext> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mover> </mrow> <mn>3</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>

<math> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mover> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mrow> <mo>+</mo> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </mover> <mover> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>O</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mover> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>

</details>

### Q92. JEE Main 2014 (12 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Which one of the following complexes will most likely absorb visible light?
(At nos. $\mathrm{Sc}=21, \mathrm{Ti}=22, \mathrm{~V}=23, \mathrm{Zn}=30$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{Sc}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{3+}$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{Ti}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{4+}$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{V}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+}$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{Zn}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{2+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\left[\mathrm{V}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The absorption of visible light and hence coloured nature of the transition metal cation is due to the promotion of one or more unpaired - $d$ - electron from a lower to higher level within same $d$-subshell. Hence higher will be the number of unpaired electron higher will be the absorpion in visible light.
The electronic configuration of the given elements is $\mathrm{Sc}^{3+}(18)=1 s^2 2 s^2 2 p^6 3 s^2 3 p^6 3 d^0 4 s^0-$ no unpaired $\mathrm{e}^{-}$.
$\mathrm{Ti}^{4+}(18)=1 s^2 2 \mathrm{~s}^2 2 p^6 3 s^2 3 p^6 3 d^0 4 s^0-$ no unpaired $\mathrm{e}^{-}$. $\mathrm{V}^{3+}(20)=1 s^2 2 s^2 2 p^6 3 s^2 3 p^6 3 d^2 4 s^0$ - Two unpaired $\mathrm{e}^{-}$. $\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}(28)=1 s^2 2 s^2 2 p^6 3 s^2 3 p^6 3 d^{10} 4 s^0-$ no unpaired $\mathrm{e}^{-}$. hence $\left[\mathrm{V}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+}$ will most likely absorb visible light.

</details>
