### Q93. JEE Main 2014 (12 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Among the following species the one which causes the highest CFSE, $\Delta_0$ as a ligand is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{CN}^{-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{NH}_3$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{F}^{-}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{CO}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\mathrm{CO}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Following is the order of increasing value of $\Delta_0$ for the ligands.
$$
\begin{aligned}
&\mathrm{I}^{-} < \mathrm{Br}^{-} < \mathrm{Cl}^{-} < \mathrm{F}^{-} < \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O} < \mathrm{Py}=\mathrm{NH}_3 < \text { en } \\
& < \mathrm{NO}_2-< < \mathrm{CN}^{-} < \mathrm{CO}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q94. JEE Main 2014 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

An octahedral complex of Co<sup>3</sup><sup>+</sup> is diamagnetic. The hybridisation involved in the formation of the complex is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** dsp<sup>2</sup>
- **(B)** d<sup>2</sup>sp<sup>3</sup>
- **(C)** dsp<sup>3</sup>d
- **(D)** sp<sup>3</sup>d<sup>2</sup>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) d<sup>2</sup>sp<sup>3</sup>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math> <mrow> <msub> <mspace></mspace> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </msub> <msup> <mtext>Co</mtext> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>+</mo> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> has 24 Electrons i.e 6 electrons in inner 3d orbital of central metal ion for diamagnetic behaviour these d<sup>6</sup> Electrons have to be paired and due to pairing two inner d-orbitals get empty and hybridisation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/EM0026103DUP01HIN.jpg" style="width: 459px; height: 136px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q95. JEE Main 2014 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

A compound of vanadium chloride has spin only magnetic moment of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn></math> BM. Its formula is

**Options:**

- **(A)** VCl<sub>2</sub>
- **(B)** VCl<sub>5</sub>
- **(C)** VCl<sub>4</sub>
- **(D)** VCl<sub>3</sub>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) VCl<sub>4</sub>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Spin only Magnetic Moment

<math> <mrow> <mtext>&#956;</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mtext>n</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>n</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </msqrt> <mtext>&#160;BM</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>7</mn> <mn>3</mn> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> <mo>&#160;n</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn>&#160; &#160;&#160;</mrow></math> (unpaired e<sup>-</sup>)

Electronic configuration of <math> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext><mo>=</mo><msup> <mrow> <mtext>1s</mtext></mrow> <mtext>2</mtext> </msup> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>2s</mtext></mrow> <mtext>2</mtext> </msup> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>2p</mtext></mrow> <mtext>6</mtext> </msup> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>3s</mtext></mrow> <mtext>2</mtext> </msup> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>3p</mtext></mrow> <mtext>6</mtext> </msup> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>3d</mtext></mrow> <mtext>3</mtext> </msup> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>4s</mtext></mrow> <mtext>2</mtext> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/EM0025985_AnsExp_11.jpg" style="width: 328px; height: 68px;" />

to get 1 unpaired electron <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> has to lose 4 electrons to form <math><mrow><msup><mtext>V</mtext> <mrow> <mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>.</mo></math>

So, formula of chloride is <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mtext>VCl</mtext></mrow> <mtext>4</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q96. JEE Main 2014 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The correct statement about the magnetic properties of [Fe(CN)<sub>6</sub>]<sup>3-</sup> and [FeF<sub>6</sub>]<sup>3-</sup> is : (Z = 26).

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both are paramagnetic.
- **(B)** Both are diamagnetic.
- **(C)** [Fe(CN)<sub>6</sub>]<sup>3-  </sup>is paramagnetic,

 [FeF<sub>6</sub>]<sup>3- </sup>is diamagnetic.
- **(D)** [Fe(CN)<sub>6</sub>]<sup>3-  </sup>is diamagnetic,

[FeF<sub>6</sub>]<sup>3- </sup>is paramagnetic.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both are paramagnetic.**

**Detailed Solution:**

In both complexes Fe is in +3 oxidation state.
F<sup>-</sup> ion  is weak field ligand so no pairing of electrons in inner 3d-orbitals &amp; outer orbital complex. sp<sup>3</sup>d<sup>2</sup> hybridisation is formed.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/EM0025961_AnsExp_1.jpg" style="width: 472px; height: 104px;" />
have 5 unpaired electrons, so paramagnetic

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>35</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></msqrt><mtext>B</mtext><mo>.</mo><mtext>M</mtext></math>
​​
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/EM0025961_AnsExp_2.jpg" style="opacity: 0.9; width: 369px; height: 87px;" />
One unpaired Electron, CN<sup>-</sup> ion strong ligand field. So will pair the d-orbital electrons &amp; form inner orbital hybridization d<sup>2</sup>sp<sup>3</sup> .

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>&#956;</mtext> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>Fe</mtext> <msub> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>CN</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>6</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>-</mo> </mrow> </msup> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mn>1</mn> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn>&#160; &#160;&#160;</mrow></mfenced></msqrt></mrow></math>    (1 unpaired e<sup>-</sup> so para magnetic)

                        <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> <mo>B.M</mo> </mrow> </math>.

</details>

### Q97. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The octahedral complex of a metal ion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> with four monodentate ligands <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math><sub> </sub>absorb wavelengths in the region of red, green, yellow and blue, respectively. The increasing order of ligand strength of the four ligands is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The colors of wavelengths are absorbed in decreasing order of wavelengths.

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mtext>red</mtext> </msub> <mo>&#62;</mo> <msub> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mtext>yellow</mtext> </msub> <mo>&#62;</mo> <msub> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mtext>green</mtext> </msub> <mo>&#62;</mo> <msub> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mtext>blue</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </math>

as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mfrac></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mtext>E</mtext> <mtext>Red</mtext> </msub> <mo>&#60;</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mtext>E</mtext> <mtext>Yellow</mtext> </msub> <mo>&#60;</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mtext>E</mtext> <mtext>Green</mtext> </msub> <mo>&#60;</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mtext>E</mtext> <mtext>Blue</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>

As the order of ligand field strength provides the order of relative crystal field splitting energies.

</details>

### Q98. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In which of the following octahedral complex species the magnitude of $\Delta_0$ will be maximum?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{O}_4\right)_3\right]^{3-}$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Crystal field splitting depends upon the nature of ligand. The nature of ligand $\Delta$ decreases as shown below
$\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{O}_4^{--} < \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O} < \mathrm{NH}_3 < \mathrm{CN}^{-}$
hence the crystal field splitting will be maximum for $\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}$

</details>

---

### Q99. JEE Main 2013 (23 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The magnetic moment of the complex anion $\left[\mathrm{Cr}(\mathrm{NO})\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)(\mathrm{CN})_4\right]^{2-}$ is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5.91 \mathrm{BM}$
- **(B)** $3.87 \mathrm{BM}$
- **(C)** $1.73 \mathrm{BM}$
- **(D)** $2.82 \mathrm{BM}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2.82 \mathrm{BM}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\operatorname{In}\left[\mathrm{Cr}(\mathrm{NO})\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)(\mathrm{CN})_4\right]^{2-}$, $\mathrm{Cr}^{2+}\left(\mathrm{d}^4\right)$ is given as :
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/M-AzxfSV8cYMj3ikB4UxGObeMr7WsXXxjynMLDV4A7s.original.fullsize.png">

i.e., 2 unpaired electrons
$$
\mu=\sqrt{2(2+2)}=\sqrt{8}=2.82
$$

</details>

### Q100. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following statements is incorrect?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}$ ion also gives blood red colour with $\mathrm{SCN}^{-}$ion.
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}$ ion also gives blood red colour with $\mathrm{SCN}^{-}$ion.
- **(C)** On passing $\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{~S}$ into $\mathrm{Na}_2 \mathrm{ZnO}_2$ solution a white ppt of $\mathrm{ZnS}$ is formed.
- **(D)** Cupric ion reacts with excess of ammonia solution to give deep blue colour of $\left[\mathrm{Cu}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4\right]^{2+}$ ion.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}$ ion also gives blood red colour with $\mathrm{SCN}^{-}$ion.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Only $\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}$ ions give blood red colouration with $\mathrm{SCN}^{-}$ions.
$$
\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}+\mathrm{SCN}^{-} \longrightarrow \begin{gathered}
{[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{SCN})]^{2+}} \\
\text { (dark red) }
\end{gathered}
$$

</details>

### Q101. JEE Main 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In which of the following octahedral complexes of Co (at. no. 27), will the magnitude of $\Delta_{\circ}$ be the highest?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{O}_4\right)_3\right]^{3-}$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{3+}$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{CN}^{\Theta}$ is stronger ligand hence $\Delta_{\circ}$ is highest.

</details>
